    295:### NCTX-02 — A diagonal representation tests the withdrawn faithful-flatness claim
    299:Over a field, let G=G_m and N=span(1,t,t^{-1}) inside k[t,t^{-1}], with the left regular action. This finite saturated stable module generates the coordinate algebra, but G→GL(N) is z↦diag(1,z^{-1},z), which is faithful and has image in a proper diagonal subgroup. A nonzero off-diagonal coordinate function on GL_3 maps to zero, so the coordinate-ring map is not injective and hence cannot be faithfully flat. This tests the cancelled claim only and leaves the live faithful-representation argument intact.

    305:Let R=k[t]_(t), K=k(t), and take the open subgroup G=Spec(R)⊔Spec(K) of the constant two-element group over Spec(R), retaining the whole identity component and only the generic point of the other component. It is flat, affine and of finite presentation, but the identity subgroup U contains the entire special fibre and is not G, so condition R fails. Its algebra B=R×K has a finite R-submodule N=0×R whose saturation is 0×K, which is not finite over R; the nonidentity irreducible component also misses the closed fibre. The example demonstrates both obstructions in the same object, is consistent with the finite-type criterion on 90/010, and supplies no counterexample to that criterion.

    299:Over a field, let G=G_m and N=span(1,t,t^{-1}) inside k[t,t^{-1}], with the left regular action.     305:Let R=k[t]_(t), K=k(t), and take the open subgroup G=Spec(R)⊔Spec(K) of the constant two-element group over Spec(R), retaining the whole identity component and only the generic point of the other component.
